industrialization pathways, accelerating the joint construction of an innovation and industrial ecosystem for nuclear
fusion energy. As China’s first enterprise to achieve large-scale production of powder metallurgy tool steel materials,
the Group has successfully integrated into the national nuclear fusion innovation chain. This demonstrates the
Group’s professional reputation in high-reliability materials for extreme environments. The Group has also become
a member unit of the Joint Laboratory (the “Joint Laboratory”) led by the Institute of Energy, Hefei Comprehensive
National Science Center. It is tackling key technological challenges and conducting engineering verification for
material bottlenecks faced by magnetic confinement fusion devices, such as intense neutron irradiation, high thermal
loads and long-term stability. Key projects include ton-scale manufacturing of large-dimension 304B7 materials,
addressing challenges in composition uniformity, structural stability and synergistic strength-toughness; and R&D
of novel neutron shielding steel with boron content exceeding 3.2wt%, achieving integrated shielding and load-
bearing functions. The Group’s participation in the Joint Laboratory represents a significant step for corporate’s
deeply engaging with national nuclear fusion initiatives and strategically positioning itself for future energy materials.
In addition, the Group is expected to deeply participate, through its membership unit in the Joint Laboratory, in the
International Thermonuclear Experimental Reactor (ITER) project under construction in France, and support the
development and delivery of multiple core procurement packages, including key neutron shielding materials such as
304B7 nuclear-grade high-boron steel, thereby further consolidating the Group’s strategic position in the global high-
end nuclear power materials supply chain.

Cash Conversion Cycle
The cash conversion cycle, calculated as turnover days over inventory, plus turnover days over trade receivables,
minus turnover days of trade payables, attempts to measure the amount of time each net input dollar is tied up
in the production and sales process before it is converted into cash through sales to customers. It is important to
manufacturers because it measures the efficiency of their capital chain management.


The Group’s turnover days of inventory for 2025 was 247 days (2024: 237 days). The increase in inventory turnover
days was primarily attributable to the upward trend in the price of tungsten, the Group’s key raw material, which
elevated the carrying value of inventory. This, combined with a short-term lag in sales outbound velocity, led to a
temporary rise in turnover days. The Group will continue to enhance inventory management and adjust procurement
schedules as appropriate.


The Group’s turnover days of trade receivables for 2025 was 233 days (2024: 216 days) while the turnover days
of trade payables for 2025 was 114 days (2024: 116 days). The increase in turnover days of trade receivables was
mainly attributable to the Group’s provision of temporary credit term support to strategic customers in Europe in
response to the market downturn. The Group will continue to monitor the operating status of these customers and
adjust credit terms dynamically.


Accordingly, the Group’s cash conversion cycle for 2025 was 366 days (2024: 337 days). The management will
continue to monitor closely the operations in view of the changing business environment.


Caution: It should be noted that the calculation of the aforesaid indexes may not be consistent with those
measurement indexes published by other issuers.


Capital Expenditure and Capital Commitments
The Group adopts prudent treasury policies by closely monitoring the Group’s liquidity position to ensure that the
internal resources and standby banking facilities can meet the funding requirements of its daily operations and any
demands for capital in the future development.


For 2025, the Group’s net decrease in property, plant and equipment amounted to RMB64,267,000. Major capital

capital commitments amounted to RMB535,009,000 (2024: RMB734,949,000), of which RMB207,601,000 (2024:

contracted for. The majority of capital commitments related to expansion of titanium rod, wire and powder production
line, and new screw tap production line etc. and will be funded by internal resources and operating cash flows of the
Group.

put in place measures such as monthly review of product pricing in the light of foreign exchange fluctuations and
incentivising overseas customers to settle balances on a more timely basis to minimise the financial impact of
exchange rate exposures.

     Principal Risks and Uncertainties and Mitigating Measures
     In addition to the financial risks mentioned above, the Group faces certain operating risks and uncertainties, including
     downstream demand, geopolitical tensions in the Middle East and business expansion.


     Uncertainties in downstream demand fluctuations and macroeconomic recovery
     The Group’s high-end materials are widely used in sectors such as consumer electronics, new energy vehicles and
     high-end equipment manufacturing. Although domestic manufacturing demand gradually recovered in 2025, any
     reversal in macroeconomic recovery or persistent weakness in end-consumer spending would directly impact the
     Group’s order growth and capacity utilization, potentially exerting downward pressure on product pricing. To address
     this, the Group is actively expanding into multiple emerging sectors to effectively diversify and hedge against
     operational risks arising from demand fluctuations in any single downstream market.

Geopolitical conflicts in the Middle East, resulting in energy price surges and uncertainties in the
raw material supply chain
The US-Israel-Iran conflict that emerged in 2026 has caused energy price spikes and disrupted global supply chain
resilience, impacting global manufacturing. Although China’s overall energy supply is not heavily dependent on
imported oil, making the impact on domestic manufacturing relatively controllable, the global increase in electricity
costs, material expenses and transportation costs will inevitably push up overall manufacturing costs. Should
domestic demand in China remain persistently weak, despite having relatively strong pricing power and cost-passing
ability as a high-tech barrier high-end manufacturer, the Group still faces the risk of margin compression.


To effectively hedge against this uncertainty, the Group is accelerating capacity expansion at its Thailand precision
tools plant and tool & die steel base. By building a more resilient overseas localized production and delivery network,
the Group can flexibly mitigate tariff and logistics cost pressures arising from international trade frictions, thereby
ensuring steady growth in global market share. At the same time, the Group is promoting energy conservation and
emission reduction through digitalization and smart manufacturing, while strengthening product and customer mix
management and enhancing product performance to increase pricing power.


Uncertainties in new business expansion and commercialization of high-end materials
In recent years, the Group has accelerated its transformation into the high-end new materials sector, achieving
major technological breakthroughs in cutting-edge fields such as humanoid robotics components, nuclear fusion
materials and 3D printing titanium powder. However, these high-end materials businesses are currently mostly in the
technology validation or small-batch delivery stage. The subsequent pace of large-scale commercialization is highly
dependent on the development trajectory of downstream emerging industries and the timing of customer adoption.
If downstream customers slow down technology upgrades due to demand or cost considerations, or if the market
penetration rate of emerging industries falls short of expectations, the Group may face delays in new product sales
and profit realization.


In Compliance with Laws and Regulations
As a company listed in Hong Kong, the Company is subject to the regulations of the Securities and Futures
Commission of Hong Kong and The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited (hereinafter referred to as the “Stock
Exchange”). Since the shares of TG Tech, a 67.63% owned subsidiary of the Company, are listed on the Beijing
Stock Exchange (the “BSE”), TG Tech is subject to the regulations of the BSE. Also, various PRC government
authorities, particularly the State Environmental Protection Administration, have the authority to issue and implement
regulations governing various aspects of special steel production. The Group will constantly update and ensure
compliance with new rules and regulations issued by these regulators.


As at 31 December 2025 and up to the date of this report, the Board was unaware of any non-compliance with the
relevant laws and regulations that had a significant impact on the Group.

     Relationships with Key Stakeholders
     (i)    Employees
            The Group recognises human resources as assets important to the Group’s development and growth. Most
            of the Group’s employees were working at the manufacturing plant in Zhenjiang City, the PRC. The Group
            provided employees with remuneration packages comparable to the market rates and employees were further
            rewarded based on their performance according to the framework of the Group’s salaries, incentives and
            bonuses scheme. In order to enhance the Group’s productivity, and further improve the quality of the Group’s
            human resources, the Group provided compulsory continuous training for all of its staff on a regular basis.


            As at 31 December 2025, the gender ratio of the Group’s workforce (including the Company’s senior
            management) was approximately 69.3% male to 30.7% female. The Company’s hiring is merit-based and non-
            discriminatory. Due to the nature of the businesses involved, it is common for there to be a higher number of
            male employees hired and therefor the Board is satisfied that the Company has achieved gender diversity in its
            workforce.


     (ii)   Suppliers
            The Group’s suppliers mainly supplied us with raw materials and utilities in the PRC. The Group developed
            longstanding and good relationships with its suppliers. Our procurement department conducted regular
            meetings with the suppliers of raw materials and utilities to ensure that raw materials supplied and utilities
            were in stable supply and of good quality.


     (iii) Customers
            The Group established long-term cooperation relationships with major customers. The sales team in domestic
            and overseas sales offices visit and discuss with customers regularly so as to keep us well-informed of market
            information and changes.

     1.   About this ESG Report
          1.1 Basis of Preparation
               This report is the tenth Environmental, Social and Governance report (hereinafter referred to as “this ESG
               Report”) issued by Tiangong International Company Limited (hereinafter referred to as “the Company”)
               and its subsidiaries (hereinafter collectively referred to as “the Group”), which systematically presents
               the Group’s performance in environmental, social and governance (ESG) aspects for the year 2025.


               The Company prepared this ESG Report in accordance with Appendix C2 Environmental, Social and
               Governance Reporting Code (hereinafter referred to as the “ESG Code”) of the Rules Governing the
               Listing of Securities on The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited.


          1.2 Scope of the Report
               This ESG Report covers the Company and its major subsidiaries. The reporting period is aligned with the
               2025 annual report, from 1 January 2025 to 31 December 2025 (the reporting period). To enhance the
               narrative completeness of the report, some content may fall outside the above scope.


          1.3 Reporting Principles
               “Importance” Principle
               The Group identifies and confirms materiality topics applicable to the Group for the reporting period, and
               discloses them as key topics in this ESG Report.


               “Quantification” Principle
               To comprehensively assess the Group’s ESG performance during the reporting period, the Group
               discloses the quantitative key performance indicators applicable under the ESG Code, and specifies the
               standards, methods, assumptions, and references used to calculate the quantitative KPIs, including the
               sources of the primary conversion factors.


               “Consistency” Principle
               Unless otherwise stated, this ESG Report uses the same preparation and data calculation methods as the
               previous reporting period to enable readers to make meaningful comparisons of the ESG information in
               this report.


               “Balance” Principle
               This ESG Report follows the balance principle, objectively presenting the Group’s ESG performance and
               management status during the reporting period.

2   Statement of Board of Directors
    The Group highly recognizes the importance of ESG for the long-term and sound operation of the company.
    The Group strictly follows the ESG Code, continuously improving the Group’s ESG management system and
    oversight mechanisms. The Board of Directors (the “Board”) is the highest responsible, decision-making and
    supervisory body for ESG matters, mainly responsible for ESG-related management and for supervising and
    coordinating the Group’s ESG risk management.


    The Group regularly conducts materiality assessments on ESG topics. The specific assessment process and
    results are detailed in the ESG Report in the chapter on “Materiality Assessment”. The Board has assessed
    the potential impact and opportunities of ESG-related matters on the Group’s overall strategy and has reviewed
    the materiality assessment results of ESG topics. The Board is fully informed about the Group’s ESG-related
    issues and confirms that the current management policies and philosophy align with the Group’s sustainable
    development strategy. Details of the Group’s important ESG topics in environmental and social aspects are
    disclosed in this ESG Report.


    The Board continually strengthens its supervision and participation in the Group’s ESG governance, and reviews
    progress and outcomes of ESG-related targets annually. The Group continuously monitors the progress and
    completion of targets related to greenhouse gas emissions, energy and resource use efficiency, and waste
    management, and discloses them in this ESG Report.


    This ESG Report is true, accurate, and complete, with no false records, misleading statements, or material
    omissions.

     3   Strengthening ESG Management
         3.1 ESG Governance Structure
              The Board is the Group’s highest authority for ESG matters, providing comprehensive supervision
              over the Group’s ESG-related items and responsible for formulating and reviewing the Group’s ESG
              development strategy and targets. The Board leads and participates in the identification, assessment,
              clarification and review of ESG risks (including climate change) and related matters, establishing a
              potential ESG risk list, and ensuring appropriate and effective risk management and internal control
              measures through robust risk management and internal control processes. Under the Board’s leadership,
              the Group strictly complies with the laws and regulations of the regions where it operates in major
              decision-making processes, and, in line with the Group’s actual situation, formulates internal charters,
              policies, and procedures related to ESG matters to ensure standardized and effective management and
              implementation.


              In addition, to further integrate ESG concepts into corporate governance and comprehensively enhance
              the Group’s ESG management capabilities, the Board has established an ESG Working Group, chaired by
              the Group’s Directors, the Chief Financial Officer and the Finance Director, with members appointed by
              the Group’s ESG-related departments and liaison representatives from the Company’s subsidiaries.


              ESG Working Group responsibilities include:


              ➢     Assisting the Board in coordinating and supervising the Group’s ESG policies (including but not
                    limited to environmental protection, climate change, human rights, health and safety, business
                    ethics, information security, etc.) and the implementation of related ESG strategies.


              ➢     Reviewing and assessing the latest developments and trends in ESG and sustainability.


              ➢     Continuously monitoring the latest developments and trends in industry sustainable development.


              ➢     Reporting to the Board on material ESG matters and potential ESG risks (including climate change).


              ➢     Regularly collecting ESG-related data and information, and coordinating the preparation and
                    disclosure of the annual ESG report.

3.3 Materiality Assessment
    To gain a full understanding of stakeholders’ concerns, respond to their expectations and demands, and
    incorporate stakeholders’ recommendations into our ESG management practices, the Group regularly
    conducts ESG materiality assessment and analysis, and reviews and audits the assessment results.


    ESG Materiality Analysis Process:


    Identification of Issues
    ➢    Refer to the HKEx ESG Code and capital market ratings, benchmark against peers’ best practices,
         identify and organize material issues spanning governance and the three dimensions of economic,
         social, and environmental factors, forming our own issue library.


    Stakeholder Survey
    ➢    Identify key stakeholder groups and the methods and channels for communicating with them.


    ➢    Invite key internal and external stakeholders to complete online or offline survey questionnaires, and
         prioritize the ESG topics in the topic bank by their level of impact.


    Analysis and Evaluation
    ➢    Consolidate questionnaire feedback and survey results.


    ➢    Rank issues by maturity, stakeholder attention, and their importance to the Group.


    ➢    Derive a materiality matrix, using the matrix analysis to identify truly material issues.


    Review and Confirmation
    ➢    Confirm material issues in conjunction with expert opinions.


    ➢    The Board reviews and confirms high-impact material issues, and discloses them prominently in the
         annual ESG report.

     4   Enhancing Corporate Governance
         The Group has always regarded improving corporate governance as the core cornerstone of sustainable
         business development, building a systematic, transparent, and compliant governance framework through
         sound internal controls, clear rights and responsibilities, and efficient supervision processes.


         4.1 Fortifying Risk Defense
              The Group continues to enhance its risk management framework. The Board bears ultimate responsibility
              for risk management in the course of operations. Its Audit Committee is responsible for monitoring the
              sound operation of the risk management and internal control systems, for reviewing the effectiveness of
              controls over the Company’s major risks, and for overall risk oversight. Management is accountable to the
              Board and responsible for implementing risk management.


              The first line of                                    Business units and subsidiaries: formulate risk control measures in accordance
                                defense                            with risk management standards and integrate them into daily risk management.
              The second line of                                   Legal Department and various risk management functions: assist and oversee
                                defense                            the implementation of risk management policies by each department and promptly
                                                                   identify risk gaps.
              The third line of                                    Internal Audit Department and Audit Committee: independent from other
                                defense                            departments, conduct risk monitoring and audits, and assess the effectiveness of
                                                                   risk controls.
                                                                                 The Group’s Three Lines of Defense

The Group has established a comprehensive risk management process to identify, assess, and respond
to risks effectively.


Risk identification     Identifying potential sources of risk through collecting information and categorizing
                        them according to risk attributes.
Risk assessment         Assessing risk levels based on likelihood and severity, and prioritizing risks by
                        significance.
Risk response           Establishing risk response measures at department level and continuously
                        evaluating their effectiveness.
Risk monitoring         Continuously assessing changes in risks, and monitoring the progress and
                        effectiveness of risk mitigation measures.

The Group conducts internal audits regularly at its operating locations to prevent and control risks.
During the reporting period, the Group organized risk management-related audits, covering internal risk
assessments in areas such as quality, occupational health and safety, environment, and compliance
system standards. At the same time, the Group conducted external audits upon request by users,
covering labor, occupational health and safety, environment, and business ethics.


The Group is committed to fostering a culture of risk management with everyone involved. Through
broad risk and compliance awareness training, the Group actively promotes the continuous enhancement
of employees’ risk awareness and compliance capabilities. Each year, the Group arranges for directors to
attend training organized by regulatory authorities, covering topics such as directors’ legal responsibilities,
internal controls for listed companies, interpretation of regulatory policies, and analyses of typical
disciplinary cases, to help directors strengthen risk awareness and risk identification capability. When
developing new products and new business relationships, the Group takes into consideration risks
including financial risk, regulatory risk, and operational risk. In addition, the Group incorporates risk
management indicators into the performance assessment of senior management to enhance their risk-
bearing awareness.

         4.2 Strengthening Compliance and Internal Controls
              The Group has established a comprehensive compliance and internal control management framework,
              including policies such as the Internal Audit System and the Contract Management System. Through the
              coordination of business units, the Legal Department, and the Internal Audit Department, and ongoing
              optimization of norms and processes, the Group strictly prevents any conduct that could harm the
              Group’s interests and safeguards its legitimate rights and interests.


              The Internal Audit Department is responsible for auditing key matters such as internal controls, financial
              status, and trade security, and for proposing improvements. To ensure impartiality and objectivity, internal
              audit staff rotate every three to five years. The Group has established a positive communication and
              cooperation mechanism with the People’s Procuratorate of Danyang City, and set up the “Jian ‘Dan’
              Dang” private-enterprise compliance service station within the Group, continually raising the level of
              standardized corporate compliance governance.

4.3 Upholding Business Ethics
    The Group adheres to the principle of integrity, with zero tolerance for any illegal acts such as corruption,
    bribery, extortion, money laundering, and fraud. The Group strictly complies with the laws and regulations
    including the Criminal Law of the People’s Republic of China, the Anti-Money Laundering Law of the PRC,
    and the Anti-Unfair Competition Law of the PRC, and, in conjunction with the regulatory requirements
    where business operations are located, formulates the Code of Business Conduct to enhance
    employees’ awareness of business ethics. The Group has also established management measures
    such as the Employee Integrity and Self-Discipline Management Regulations, clarifying the meanings
    and requirements across multiple aspects including discrimination, harassment, conflicts of interest,
    insider trading, bribery and corruption, fair competition, and social responsibility. The Group requires
    all employees to sign the Employee Integrity and Self-Discipline Commitment Letter, ensuring every
    employee complies with laws and regulations, clearly understands their responsibilities, and safeguards
    the mutual interests of the Group and its employees.


    The Group’s Internal Audit Department conducts regular audits related to business ethics, promptly
    identifying potential business ethics risks and issues, proposing improvements, and checking remediation
    to ensure business activities meet ethical standards. The Group has established a business ethics
    reporting mechanism and the Management Regulations on Complaints, Reporting and Appeals,
    encouraging employees and other stakeholders to actively report behaviors that violate business ethics.
    The Internal Audit Department is responsible for report management. The Group allows anonymous
    reporting and treats report information as strictly confidential. Upon receiving a report, the Group will
    follow the steps of intake and registration, intake evaluation and grading, establishment of an investigation
    team, conducting the investigation, forming conclusions and handling recommendations, follow-up
    remediation, and archiving, and will handle the matter in accordance with laws and regulations. The Group
    also provides training on reporting channels for employees and suppliers. In addition, the Group protects
    employees or stakeholders who file complaints, with oversight by employee representatives and the
    union, and strictly prohibits any retaliation or discriminatory actions such as punishment or dismissal. For
    employees who violate the Code of Business Ethics, the Group will take internal disciplinary measures
    according to the nature and severity of the violation.


    During the reporting period, the Group did not receive any non-compliance (including corruption,
    harassment, discrimination, etc.) reports or complaints, nor did it experience any incidents of corruption,
    bribery, discrimination or harassment, client privacy data leakage, conflicts of interest, money laundering
    or insider trading violations involving itself or its employees.

4.4 Ensuring Information Security
    The Group strictly adheres to laws and regulations such as the Cybersecurity Law of the People’s
    Republic of China, the Data Security Law of the People’s Republic of China, and the Personal Information
    Protection Law of the People’s Republic of China. During the reporting period, it revised the Information
    Security Management System, adding privacy and personal information protection-related provisions,
    clarifying requirements in areas such as data minimization, consent and notice for information collection,
    and encryption and de-identification of sensitive information.


    The Audit Committee of the Group assumes responsibility for overseeing information security. The Group
    has established the Information Security Management Leadership Group, responsible for information
    security management leadership work. The Chief Financial Officer (CFO) serves as the group leader,
    responsible for implementing information security-related tasks. The Data Center is responsible for the
    daily security management and operation and maintenance of information systems, and assists in the
    formulation, revision, and improvement of information security regulations.


    The Group adopts comprehensive data security measures, including local, off-site, and private cloud
    backups, restricting access rights for external visitors. These are supplemented by real-time data
    protection through firewalls and vulnerability scanning. At the same time, the Group has achieved an
    effective separation between the business network and the internal network to prevent potential security
    threats from penetrating, thereby enhancing the reliability of the network environment in supporting
    business operations. During the reporting period, the Group continued to improve the data backup
    system, implementing daily incremental backup, weekly full backup, and an off-site backup mechanism to
    ensure data security and recoverability. The Group has established information security-related business
    continuity plans and conducts internal audits of the information security management system annually.
    In response to information security incidents, vulnerabilities, or suspicious activities, the Group has
    established an employee reporting process to ensure that relevant incidents are properly addressed.


    The Group conducts annual information security training for employees in key positions. The training
    covers requirements for the secure use of information systems, information confidentiality standards,
    etc. During the reporting period, the Group did not experience data leakage, invasion of customer privacy,
    or other information security-related incidents.

                                                                                          Annual Report 2025     47
     ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT




     5   Quality Leads to an Excellent Future
         Driven by innovation as the engine of R&D, and excellence as the standard of quality, the Group deeply
         integrates the philosophy of striving for perfection into the entire product lifecycle, builds a full-chain quality
         control system, and forges future-oriented sustainable competitive advantage.


         5.1 Stimulating the Momentum of Innovation
              The Group vigorously supports scientific research and innovation, establishing a systematic framework
              of tiered management, tiered evaluation, and tiered rewards, and formulating a series of policies and
              management measures related to scientific and technological innovation, thereby stimulating employees’
              innovative potential and promoting the application and transformation of scientific and technological
              innovation outcomes. During the reporting period, the Group’s R&D expenditure was RMB287.58 million,
              accounting for over 6% of total annual revenue.


              •     R&D Innovation Management
                    The Group has formulated supporting systems for scientific and technological innovation, including
                    the Measures for the Administration of Science and Technology Innovation, the Regulations for
                    the Management of Scientific Research Projects, and the Rationalization Proposals Management
                    System. During the reporting period, it revised the Management Measures for the Reward
                    of Science and Technology Innovation and the Measures for the Management of Technology
                    Innovation Projects, significantly enhancing the rewards for scientific and technological
                    achievements and improving the R&D evaluation system. The Group strengthens innovation
                    incentives by raising the standards for invention patent rewards, establishing a Special Prize
                    for Major Technological Breakthroughs, and implementing an industrialization revenue-sharing
                    mechanism.


                    The Group continues to improve its innovation management system, building a research and
                    development decision-making framework centered on the Technical Committee, forming a
                    closed loop from strategic planning to the commercialization of results. The Technical Committee
                    comprises management and technology heads, responsible for setting strategy, reviewing projects,
                    and deciding key research directions. At the same time, the Group strengthens cross-department
                    collaboration and coordination, optimizes the end-to-end project management system of “initiation
                    — tracking — evaluation — transformation,” and enhances the development of technical platforms,
                    continually improving materials R&D capabilities and driving technological innovation to be more
                    closely aligned with market needs, providing core support for the Group’s high-quality development.




     Tiangong International Company Limited
48   Annual Report 2025
     ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT




    In order to foster a company-wide culture of innovation, the Group has held the Annual Summary
    and Commendation Conference on Technological Innovation for 18 consecutive years. During the
    reporting period, 87 technological innovation projects have been evaluated and officially recognized.
    The teams and individuals who have achieved notable results and outstanding performance in
    technological innovation are commended as exemplary pioneers, in order to promote innovation and
    stimulate employees’ innovative momentum.


•   R&D Innovation Achievements
    The Group accelerates the commercialization of scientific research achievements. During
    the reporting period, the Group focused on high-performance tool materials and advanced
    manufacturing materials, actively participating in national and industry key technology R&D projects.
    It conducted targeted research on microstructure homogenization control of high-performance
    high-speed steel materials and the development of wear-resistant and high-toughness cutting tool
    materials, overcoming challenges in controlling the microstructure of high-carbon high-alloy steel
    materials during smelting, solidification, and hot processing. By optimizing material design and
    processes, the Group continues to improve material performance stability and product lifespan.

    In early 2025, the Group’s TGE23 series products passed certification by the North American Die
    Casting Association (NADCA), becoming the first domestic company to meet the material standards
    for large die-casting molds. This achievement marks the Group’s leading position in the world tool
    and mould steel sector, and further demonstrates our competitiveness in the international market.




                                                                Tiangong International Company Limited
                                                                                     Annual Report 2025     49
     ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT




                    During the reporting period, the Group also achieved several honors in innovative R&D: Jiangsu
                    Tiangong Precision Tools Company Limited (“Precision Tools”) was approved as a Jiangsu Province
                    Enterprise Technology Center; Jiangsu Tiangong Technology Company Limited (“TG Tech”)
                    maintained its qualification as a national high-tech enterprise; Jiangsu Tiangong Aihe Technology
                    Company Limited (“TG Aihe”) was awarded titles such as “China’s Good Technology” and Jiangsu
                    Province’s “Specialized, Refined, Distinctive and Innovative” SMEs. Our four technical products,
                    including “High-Strength, High-Toughness, Corrosion-Resistant Titanium and Titanium Alloys” and
                    “High-Strength High-Speed Steel Micro-Wire for Aerospace Micro-Drills”, were included in the
                    Industry and Information Technology Department of Jiangsu “Two New” technology product lists.

     Case: New Breakthroughs in Powder Metallurgy

     At the 7th Asia Powder Metallurgy International Conference, the Group’s subsidiary TG Aihe,
     with the technology breakthroughs and industrialization results of the “Integrated large-mold
     built-in cooling channel powder metallurgy key component project”, won the Powder Metallurgy
     Innovation Award. Chief Scientist Dr. Yu Yang received the Powder Metallurgy Outstanding
     Contribution Award. The project overcomes key bottlenecks including achieving high-temperature
     strength, realizing integrated manufacturing of complex conformal cooling channels. It has been
     implemented in leading new energy vehicle companies, successfully overcoming a key bottleneck
     in high-end die-casting molds, and demonstrating the international competitiveness of China’s
     advanced manufacturing materials.


     Case: Significant Progress in High-Nitrogen Alloy Materials

     During the reporting period, TPMDM02N, a high-nitrogen alloy material independently developed
     by the Group, passed technical verification and was officially delivered to customers. The material
     employs a proprietary domestic refining technology to tackle challenges in nitrogen content control
     and purity enhancement for high-nitrogen alloys. Salt spray test results show corrosion resistance
     significantly superior to other materials, and it possesses high strength, high toughness, high wear
     resistance, and fatigue resistance. It has been progressively delivered to medical devices, high-end
     tooling, and other industries for validation and application. This breakthrough provides core material
     support for advanced equipment manufacturing and the robotics industry, and marks a major
     breakthrough in the domestic high-end alloy materials field.


•   Participation in Standard Setting

    progress. Since 2013, the Group has edited or participated in formulating a total of 13 standards
    across national, industry, group, and local levels. In the reporting period, the Group contributed to
    the publication of two national standards: “High-speed Tool Steels” and “Tool and Mould Steels”.

5.2 Accelerating Digital Transformation
    The Group drives business development through digitalization. In recent years, in smart factory
    construction, by deeply integrating digital, automated, and intelligent technologies, the Group has
    promoted overall improvements in production efficiency and product quality, building a digital system
    covering R&D, production, logistics, and services, including the Enterprise Resource Planning (ERP)
    system, Manufacturing Execution System (MES), Data Acquisition and Monitoring System (SCADA),
    intelligent inspection system, and a vertical/three-dimensional warehouse.


    During the reporting period, the Group continued to drive operational transformation and efficiency gains
    through digital strategies: by upgrading ERP and MES systems, deploying a big data platform and the
    “Smart Operations Center,” promoting data sharing and intelligent decision-making; fully implementing
    paperless offices and launching Alibaba Cloud’s “Cloud Procurement and Sales” system to achieve
    procurement digitization and process optimization; simultaneously launching the Precision Tools
    Production Management System to strengthen production scheduling, and comprehensively improving
    operational transparency, resource utilization efficiency, and levels of intelligence.


    The Group’s work in the digital field has also garnered numerous honors and recognition. The
    Group’s subsidiaries TG Tools and Jiangsu Tiangong Carbide Technology Company Limited (“Carbide
    Technology”) were recognized as Jiangsu Province Five-Star Cloud Adoption Enterprises. TG Aihe’s
    “Tiangong Powder Metallurgy Intelligent Factory,” and Carbide Technology’s “Tool Cutting Intelligent
    Manufacturing Workshop” were identified as Jiangsu Province Smart Manufacturing Demonstration
    Factories. TG Tech and Carbide Technology received certificates for the Integration of Informatization and

         5.3 Protecting Intellectual Property
              The Group places a high priority on intellectual property protection, strictly complying with the Trademark
              Law of the People’s Republic of China, the Patent Law of the People’s Republic of China, and the
              Copyright Law of the People’s Republic of China, among other IP laws. The Group has formulated
              systems and measures such as the Measures for the Administration of Science and Technology
              Innovation and the Rationalization Proposals Management System, which define the management
              responsibilities for copyrights, trademarks, patents, and related infringement handling processes. The
              Group has established a dedicated IP protection mechanism, engaged a professional law firm as a
              permanent legal advisor to prevent IP infringement. At the same time, the Group has established a good
              communication mechanism with the People’s Procuratorate of Danyang City to jointly combat trademark
              infringement and enhance the Company’s legal competitiveness. In addition, the Group regularly conducts
              IP training, promotes legitimate software usage, and strengthens employees’ awareness of IP protection
              to avoid infringing others’ IP rights.


              During the reporting period, the Group achieved fruitful results in intellectual property, filing 99 patents,
              with 43 patents granted. By the end of the reporting period, we owned a total of 253 patents.


         5.4 Lean Quality Control
              The Group pursues excellence in quality as its core mission, focusing on advancing lean manufacturing
              and continually improving product quality and service levels. The Group strictly complies with laws and
              regulations such as the Product Quality Law of the People’s Republic of China, and has established
              a series of product quality control systems, including the New Product Development Management
              Procedures, Non-conforming Product Control Procedures, Enterprise Metrology Management
              Regulations, Raw and Auxiliary Materials Management Measures, Steel Inspection and Acceptance
              Rules, and Quality Process Control Plans, which standardize every stage from development to production
              to ensure end-to-end quality control from raw material receipt to product shipment.

5.5 Enhancing Service Experience
    •    Customer Communication and Feedback
         The Group strictly complies with the Law of the People’s Republic of China on the Protection of
         Consumer Rights and Interests and other laws and regulations in the operating regions, and has
         formulated management documents such as the After-sales Service Management Regulations
         to standardize the customer complaint handling process and ensure complaints are properly
         processed. The Group has established a comprehensive online customer service system to achieve
         efficient input of customer information and real-time collection of quality feedback.


         During the reporting period, the Group further optimized the customer complaint handling process,
         forming a dual-path closed-loop management mechanism for quality-related and non-quality-related
         complaints. When a customer complaint is initiated through the Office Automation system (OA) and
         determined to be a quality issue, it will be handled by the quality or technical responsible person
         as appropriate, and archived after confirmation by the sales manager; if not a quality issue, the
         results are promptly communicated to the customer after verification, with an attached test report,
         achieving rapid response and closed-loop management.

6   Painting a Green Picture
    The Group adheres to the green development philosophy, continuously improves the environmental
    management system, strengthens internal refined control, and aims to reduce energy consumption, lower
    emissions, and prevent environmental incidents, promoting the coordinated development of business and
    ecology. As of the end of the reporting period, the Group’s subsidiary TG Tools was awarded the national and
    Jiangsu Province “Green Factory” titles, and TG Aihe and TG Tech were awarded the Jiangsu Province “Green
    Factory” title.


    6.1 Addressing Climate Change
          The Group integrates climate change response into its corporate strategy by optimizing the energy
          structure, innovating green technologies, and managing carbon emissions, actively promoting low-carbon
          transformation and sustainable development.


          •     Governance
                The Group attaches great importance to climate risk governance, relying on an ESG governance
                framework led by the Board to supervise and manage climate-related risks. The Board and the ESG
                Working Group hold at least annual reviews of climate-related topics. In addition, the Group regularly
                provides climate-change training for Board members and management to continuously enhance the
                governance bodies’ climate-related capabilities. Currently, the Group’s Board remuneration policy
                does not incorporate climate factors; the next step is to integrate climate-related performance
                indicators into the incentive system to further strengthen the Board’s strategic oversight and
                management responsibility on climate change issues.


          •     Strategy
                The Group references the ESG Code and the Conclusion on Enhancement of Climate-related
                Disclosures Under the Environmental, Social and Governance Framework. We identify climate-
                change-related risks and opportunities relevant to our operations and assess the potential financial
                impact through policy research, industry benchmarking, and incorporating expert opinions. The
                Group has formulated corresponding response measures to effectively prevent and respond to the
                impacts of climate change on operations.


                Meanwhile, the Group, based on its own business characteristics and development strategy, and
                considering the climate policy requirements of the operation location, has clarified the time horizons
                for climate risks and opportunities: short term (1–5 years), mid-term (6–10 years), and long-term (over
                10 years).

                    The Group actively implements the concept of low-carbon development, adheres to a low-
                    carbon economic model, and continuously reduces the adverse impact of operating activities
                    on the environment. The Group enhances energy management, adopts environmentally friendly
                    equipment, and advanced technologies to improve energy efficiency and reduce energy waste.
                    At the same time, the Group actively promotes the use of renewable energy such as solar power
                    to effectively reduce greenhouse gas emissions. The Group’s subsidiary TG Tools has been
                    recognized as a demonstration unit for compliance with carbon management system standards. In
                    addition, to address policy impacts such as CBAM, the Group actively conducts product-embedded
                    carbon emission calculations and verifications to further standardize carbon emission management.
                    Considering the current impacts of climate risks and opportunities, the Group has not formulated
                    a climate-related transformation plan; it will develop relevant plans as needed based on business
                    development and changes in the internal and external environment.


                    Due to the fact that the Group’s current skills, capabilities, and resources do not meet the analysis
                    requirements, the Group applies the “capabilities relief” for climate scenario analysis. The Group
                    fully recognizes the long-term nature of climate change response and that climate-related risks and
                    opportunities are subject to multiple influences from domestic and international policies, markets,
                    and technological advancements, with uncertain impacts on business. Therefore, the Group will
                    continue to monitor developments in policy and regulatory, industry best practices, and climate
                    models, and dynamically incorporate climate considerations into strategic planning to enhance
                    overall agility and business resilience. The Group has not implemented internal carbon pricing.


              •     Risk Management
                    The Group has established a comprehensive climate risk management process for all operations
                    and integrated climate risk into the overall enterprise risk management process. Compared with the
                    previous fiscal year, there has been no change in the Group’s climate risk management process.


                                                   Climate Risk Management Process


                    Risk Identification Based on the TCFD’s climate risks and opportunities checklist, relevant
                                         policies, and peer benchmarking, the company’s climate risks and
                                         opportunities list is finally determined to ensure the comprehensiveness and
                                         applicability of climate risk management.
                    Risk Assessment      Taking into account internal and external stakeholders’ opinions, assess the
                                         nature, likelihood, and impact of climate risks on the company’s operations
                                         and finances.
                    Risk Response        Based on the climate risk assessment results, the Environment, Health and
                                         Safety (“EHS”) department and relevant business units jointly discuss and
                                         formulate policies to respond to climate risks and opportunities and promote
                                         the implementation of management measures.
                    Risk Monitoring      Regularly review climate risks and opportunities and assess the effectiveness
                                         of response measures.

         6.2 Efficient Use of Resources
              The Group is committed to achieving efficient and circular use of resources and energy, guided by the
              principles of reducing, reusing, and recycling, to promote resource conservation and improve utilization
              efficiency.


              •     Raw Material Recycling
                    The Group’s smelting uses alloys and scrap steel as primary raw materials. Scrap steel is sourced
                    from internal recycling and upstream procurement, which reduces procurement costs and
                    production energy consumption through circular use. At the same time, the Group innovatively
                    adopts a short-process smelting technology, shortening production cycles and efficiently utilizing
                    alloys and scrap, ensuring product quality. This optimized process significantly improves resource
                    efficiency and reduces energy consumption, emissions, and environmental impact.




                                  Traditional Long-Process Special Steel Manufacturing




                    The Group’s Short-Process Special Steel Manufacturing with Recycle Mechanism




     Tiangong International Company Limited
64   Annual Report 2025
     ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT




    The Group has upgraded its titanium alloy smelting process and is continuously exploring production
    processes with electron beam cooling bed (EB) furnaces beyond the Vacuum Arc Remelting (VAR).
    Compared with VAR, EB furnaces do not require electrode prep, effectively remove impurities from
    raw materials, and can accept more scrap titanium, thereby improving scrap titanium recovery and
    achieving efficient circular use of titanium metal resources.




                  The Group’s Recycled-Material Production Flow Diagram


    The Group continuously improves EB furnace smelting technology, especially in returned-material
    handling and recycling input, conducting in-depth exploration and practice around different grades,
    specifications, and returned-material addition ratios. It has successively overcome issues such
    as aluminum volatilization losses and the uniformity control of oxygen elements, continuously
    optimizing the production process and enhancing the level of titanium alloy resource recycling.
    During the reporting period, the Number 2 EB furnace project completed hot testing smoothly.


•   Energy Management
    The Group has established a comprehensive energy management system, formulated the Energy
    Management Handbook, the Energy Use Management Measures, the Water and Electricity
    Conservation Management System and various other policies, set annual energy consumption
    targets, and continuously refined energy management. The Group has completed and put into
    operation an integrated energy management system, achieving real-time monitoring and visualized
    control of power equipment, auxiliary systems, and overall plant energy consumption. Through data
    analysis and energy consumption forecasting, the system can support the dynamic formulation
    and optimization of energy-saving solutions, thereby continuously improving energy management
    efficiency.


    As of the end of the reporting period, the Group has 5 subsidiaries that have successfully passed
    the annual surveillance audit for ISO 50001 Energy Management System certifications.

    During the reporting period, the Group continued to advance energy-saving upgrades and renewable
    energy utilization projects, continuously improving energy efficiency performance:


    Ø        Replacement of efficient equipment: The Group constructed a 2,000-tonne fast forging
             chamber furnace to replace the existing car-bottom heating furnace, reducing natural gas
             consumption per ton of steel by 20%.
    Ø        Lighting retrofit: The Group continued to retrofit traditional lighting fixtures with LED ones.
             As of the end of the reporting period, the proportion of LED lighting fixtures reached 85%.
    Ø        Rooftop photovoltaic installation: The Group has intensified the use of photovoltaic and
             other renewable energy sources, with a total installed solar capacity of 14.8 MW, continuously
             improving the cleanliness of energy consumption.


•   Water Resource Management
    The Group strictly adheres to the Water Law of the People’s Republic of China and other laws and
    regulations and has established a comprehensive water resources management system to promote
    lean water management. Our goal is to improve water use efficiency in production and operations,
    reduce water resource waste, and achieve rational use and recycling of water resources. During
    the reporting period, the Group planned to use 1 million cubic meters of water; through daily
    strengthened water management, actual water use was 761,000 cubic meters, achieving the water
    usage target.


    The Group conducts water risk assessments for major production sites using the World Resources
    Institute (WRI)’s Aqueduct Water Risk Atlas. Assessment dimensions include water resource
    dependence, impacts, and more. The assessment results indicate that the Group does not face
    water scarcity risks but is exposed to a high level of water quality risk.


     Assessment Entity               WRI Water Risk Level          Water Stress in 2030           Water Stress in 2050

     TG Tools                        Medium-High (2–3)             Low (<10%)                     Low (<10%)
     TG Aihe                         Medium-High (2–3)             Low (<10%)                     Low (<10%)
     Precision Tools                 Medium-High (2–3)             Low (<10%)                     Low (<10%)
     TG Tech                         Medium-High (2–3)             Low (<10%)                     Low (<10%)
     TG New Materials                Medium-High (2–3)             Low (<10%)                     Low (<10%)


    Notes:


    1.       WRI Water Risk: Low (0–1), Medium-Low (1–2), Medium-High (2–3), High (3–4), Very High (4–5).


    2.       WRI Water Stress: Low (<10%), Medium-Low (10%–20%), Medium-High (20%–40%), High (40%–80%), Very High
             (>80%).

                    The water resources for the Group’s production and operations are sourced from municipal water
                    supply and surface water. During the reporting period, the Group did not withdraw water in water-
                    scarce areas. To reduce water resource consumption, the Group implements refined water
                    resource management by regularly comparing sub-meter data with the main meter to promptly
                    detect water leakage, repair pipes and flushing systems, and replace old faucets. Water saved for
                    the year totaled about 6,000 cubic meters. At the same time, the Group refines the assessment of
                    water-saving targets and rewards part of the reduced costs as performance bonuses to employees
                    to motivate water conservation.


                    The Group actively promotes water resource recycling. By introducing professional technical
                    equipment, industrial wastewater after treatment at the sewage treatment plant meets reuse
                    standards and is reused as circulating cooling water; closed cooling tower equipment is adopted to
                    reduce evaporation; and treated water is used for ground washing, workshop makeup water, etc.,
                    using reclaimed water treatment, rainwater collection, and other devices.


                    In addition, the Group conducts quarterly water-saving training for employees, sharing advanced
                    water-saving measures to continuously raise awareness of water conservation. No material
                    incidents related to water resource usage occurred during the reporting period.


         6.3 Improving Environmental Protection Mechanisms
              The Group strictly adheres to the Environmental Protection Law of the People’s Republic of China
              and other laws and regulations, and has formulated management systems such as the Environmental
              Protection Management System, Regulations on the Prevention and Control of Wastewater, Waste Gas,
              and Noise, Environmental Supervision Management System, Environmental Monitoring Management
              System, and Management System for Environmental Protection Statistics, and publicly discloses the
              Environmental Protection Statement on its official website, aiming to reduce the adverse impact of its
              production and operations on the environment. During the reporting period, no penalties were incurred
              due to environmental violations.


              The Group continually improves its environmental protection management system, establishing an
              environmental protection management system consisting of the Group headquarters and affiliated
              companies, and assigns environmental responsibilities to individuals. The Group’s EHS Department
              serves as the core responsible unit, responsible for formulating environmental protection policies,
              implementing them, ensuring equipment operation procedures, and coordinating environmental issues
              and hazards. The general managers of the affiliated companies serve as the primary persons responsible
              for environmental protection, fully responsible for their company’s environmental work, including strategy
              development, risk monitoring, and effectiveness assessment. As of the end of the reporting period, the
              Group had 6 subsidiaries that obtained ISO 14001 environmental management system certification and
              passed the annual surveillance audit.

         6.4 Strict Control of Pollution Emissions
              The Group continues to strengthen pollution prevention and control efforts, aiming to reduce the
              emissions of wastewater, exhaust gas, and solid waste pollutants, and to avoid the environmental impact
              of its operations.


              •     Wastewater Management
                    The Group strictly complies with the Water Pollution Prevention and Control Law of the People’s
                    Republic of China, and has formulated the Sewage Treatment Measures and the Water Pollution
                    Prevention and Control Management Regulations. The Group strictly applies the requirements of
                    regulatory authorities and industry wastewater discharge standards, standardizes the wastewater
                    treatment processes, and reinforces the upgrading of wastewater treatment facilities and
                    technologies. The Group has established a complete wastewater treatment system. Industrial
                    wastewater and domestic sewage generated from production and operations are discharged into
                    a wastewater treatment plant, where they are treated to meet standards before discharge. A
                    monitoring system dynamically tracks pollutant discharges and water quality at outlets, thereby
                    strengthening the prevention of water pollution risks. During the reporting period, the Group’s
                    wastewater discharges complied with discharge regulatory requirements and achieved wastewater
                    management objectives.


              •     Exhaust Gas Management
                    The Group strictly complies with the Law of the People’s Republic of China on the Prevention
                    and Control of Atmospheric Pollution, and has formulated regulations such as the Regulations on
                    the Prevention and Control of Waste Gas, Wastewater, and Noise, and the Regulations on the
                    Prevention and Control of Atmospheric Pollution, etc., and strictly implements the requirements
                    of the Emission Standard of Air Pollutants for Steel Rolling Industry and the Emission Standard
                    of Air Pollutants for Steel Smelt Industry, thereby strengthening the management of exhaust
                    gas emissions. The Group’s exhaust pollutants mainly include nitrogen oxides, sulfur dioxide,
                    particulates, and fluorides. All smelting workshops are equipped with dusts removal devices,
                    and the exhaust gas is treated by dust removal device before discharge to meet the standards.
                    The related emission data have been linked to environmental protection authorities for real-time
                    monitoring of emissions.


                    During the reporting period, the Group increased the online monitoring coverage of exhaust
                    emission outlets to 100%, completed real-time data integration with the national regulatory
                    platform, and established an intelligent early warning and closed-loop disposal mechanism,
                    achieving a one-minute response to anomalies. By linking monitoring equipment with the operation
                    of pollution-control facilities, the Group has shifted from end-point control to full-process control.
                    The Group established a standardized operations and maintenance system of “daily inspection
                    + calibration + verification” to ensure data is authentic and traceable, and the emission data
                    consistently outperforms national standards.




     Tiangong International Company Limited
70   Annual Report 2025
     ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT




•   Waste Management
    The Group strictly complies with the Law of the People’s Republic of China on the Prevention
    and Control of Environmental Pollution by Solid Wastes, and has formulated regulations such as
    the Waste Management Regulations to strictly manage waste emissions. In addition, the Group
    regularly audits waste-generation points in the production process to identify potential sources of
    waste generation. During the reporting period, the Group formulated and achieved the following
    waste management objective: 100% compliant disposal and utilization of hazardous waste.


    The Group adopts classified disposal for general industrial waste and hazardous waste. For
    hazardous waste, such as spent emulsions, spent lubricating oil, dust removal ash, spent dust
    bags, spent lead-acid batteries, oil rags, barium-containing waste, and pickling sludge, the Group
    centrally collects and stores them in a dedicated hazardous waste warehouse, and entrusts
    qualified third-party transport and disposal entities to handle them. During the reporting period, the
    Group invested in innovative technology, employing a peel-shot blasting process to replace the
    traditional acid blasting process; by using a dry physical method to treat metal surfaces, it greatly
    reduces the generation of hazardous wastes such as spent acids and spent alkalis. In addition, this
    process enables the clean recovery of steel chips, directly recycled into production processes, and
    eliminates acid mist and acidic/alkaline wastewater discharges, while simultaneously improving
    treatment efficiency and product quality.


    For general industrial waste, the Group actively promotes recycling and reuse, continuously
    improving the rate of waste circulation. During the reporting period, the Group achieved internal
    recycling by re-melting the oxide scale generated from rolling and forging after briquetting; at the
    same time, for smelted steel slag, the Group employed magnetic separation and screening to
    efficiently recover steel scrap and alloy components, significantly increasing alloy recovery and
    effectively reducing resource waste and end-of-pipe treatment pressure.


    The Group regularly provides waste management training to employees to raise awareness of
    waste reduction and regulatory compliance.


•   Noise Control
    The Group has formulated the Noise Pollution Prevention and Control Management Regulations,
    prioritizes low-noise equipment, and is committed to reducing noise pollution. During the reporting
    period, the Group adopted comprehensive noise control measures, enclosed and damped high-
    noise equipment to achieve a noise reduction of 40–60%; installed sound barriers at the plant
    boundary and in key areas; established an equipment operation and maintenance ledger and
    optimized operating processes; provided protective earplugs to employees and conducted targeted
    training. Through real-time monitoring at monitoring points and quarterly inspections, plant boundary
    noise remains below the national standard, and there were no related complaints throughout the
    year.




                                                                Tiangong International Company Limited

              •     Environmental Protection Training
                    The Group centers on the EHS Department, EHS Administrators, and Workshop Supervisors, and
                    has systematically established and implemented a three-level environmental training system that
                    covers all staff, continuously enhancing management to frontline employees’ compliance awareness
                    and practical ability in environmental governance. The training system includes three levels:


                    •    Professional Capability Building (Special Training for EHS Officers): Led by the EHS
                         Department, 12 training sessions are held annually with a total of 256 participants. The
                         content focuses on in-depth interpretation of environmental laws and regulations, emission
                         permit management, compliant disposal of hazardous waste, environmental emergency
                         response planning, environmental ledger management, and operation and maintenance of
                         environmental facilities.


                    •    Management Awareness and Responsibility Enhancement (Company-level Safety and
                         Environmental Meetings): Adopting a “meetings-as-training” model, the meetings are
                         organized by the Group’s Safety Production Committee, EHS Department, and management,
                         with one session per month, totaling 920 participants. The meetings focus on policy
                         dissemination, setting annual environmental goals and key tasks, reporting typical issues and
                         organizing corrective actions, and reinforcing environmental management responsibilities at all
                         levels.


                    •    Grassroots Practical Ability Consolidation (Frontline On-site Training): Implemented
                         by Workshop Supervisors (who also serve as on-site EHS officers), this level features 73
                         targeted training courses conducted throughout the year, covering 1,465 participants. The
                         training content is closely aligned with production realities, including on-site environmental
                         operation procedures, key points for on-site control of wastewater, exhaust gas, and solid
                         wastes, routine operation of environmental facilities, and initial identification and reporting of
                         environmental hazards.


         6.5 Promoting Green Office
              The Group promotes green office practices by regulating the use of office equipment, turning on lighting
              as needed, and reasonably setting air conditioning temperatures, actively guiding employees to adopt
              low-carbon office concepts, and creating an energy-saving, environmentally friendly work atmosphere.


              ➢     Paperless Office: The Group actively advances a paperless office by increasing the digitization of
                    business processes to reduce paper usage. In addition, the Group strengthens centralized control
                    of printers, enabling real-time monitoring of printing paper and consumables and eliminating paper
                    waste.




     Tiangong International Company Limited
72   Annual Report 2025
           ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT




    ➢    Energy Conservation: The Group implements office energy-saving measures, uses energy-saving
         lighting, and powers night-time lighting with a photovoltaic storage system; central air conditioning
         is uniformly set to 26°C, with seasonal temperature-based operation; employees are also required
         to turn off computers and lighting in public areas when leaving.


    ➢    Procurement of New Energy Vehicles for Commuting: The Group purchases new energy buses
         and cars for employee commuting and installs charging stations within the factory area to reduce
         carbon emissions from commuting.


    ➢    Initiate Environmental Protection Publicity: The Group actively advocates the concepts of
         conservation and environmental protection, strengthens energy-saving awareness among all
         staff through internal publicity, requires employees to promptly shut off power to production and
         office equipment when leaving, and eliminates electricity waste. At the same time, the canteen
         implements the “Clean Plate Campaign,” encouraging staff to take meals according to their needs
         to reduce food waste.


6.6 Protecting Biodiversity
    The Group is committed to integrating ecological protection into its entire operation and has issued
    and publicized the Environmental Protection Statement, which includes biodiversity management
    requirements. In addition, the Group uses WWF’s Biodiversity Risk Filter to conduct biodiversity risk
    assessments, evaluating factors such as the dependence of production and operational activities on
    the ecological environment and potential impacts. Evaluation dimensions include basin physical risk,
    ecosystem provisioning services, biodiversity pressure, and basin reputational risk. The assessment
    results indicate that the Group’s main operating locations face medium-to-high biodiversity risk.


    The Group strictly adheres to national ecological red line regulations, ensuring that all construction
    projects complete environmental impact assessments and approvals in accordance with procedures.
    Through systematic checks, the Group confirms that no production facilities are located within ecological
    red line areas and there are no sites with significant biodiversity impacts. Meanwhile, the Group continues
    to promote green production transformation and site environmental optimization, strengthens controls
    on resource consumption and pollutant emissions, and, through standardized and institutional long-term
    mechanisms, maintains regional ecological balance and fulfills the primary responsibility for ecological
    protection.

     7   Strengthening the Safety Foundation
         The Group always places the life, health, and safety of its employees first, by improving the safety
         management system, enhancing safety training, and improving equipment safety, to continuously ensure safe
         production and employees’ occupational health.


         7.1 Implementing Safety Responsibilities
              The Group strictly complies with the Law of the People’s Republic of China on Work Safety, the Law
              of the People’s Republic of China on Prevention and Control of Occupational Diseases, the Interim
              Provisions on Occupational Health Supervision and Management in the Workplace and the Management
              Measures for Occupational Disease Diagnosis and Identification, and other laws and regulations. The
              Group has formulated systems including the Occupational Health Management System, the Special
              Operations Safety Management System, the Safety Production Responsibility Insurance Management
              System, and the Safety Production Reward and Punishment Management System. During the reporting
              period, the Group revised 12 core systems, including the Safety Production Responsibility System and
              the Hazard Identification and Rectification Management Measures, and added the New Equipment Safety
              Operation Procedures and the Special Management Regulations for Confined Space Operations, to fully
              safeguard employees’ life safety and physical health in production.


              The Group has established a three-level safety management framework with a company-level Safety
              Committee as the decision-making core, department/workshop-level bodies as the execution entities,
              and frontline teams/positions as the on-site foundation. The Safety Committee is led by the key person
              in charge and is responsible for formulating policies and objectives; the Safety, Environment, and Health
              Department acts as a dedicated unit coordinating daily management. Heads of production workshops
              and functional departments are the primary safety responsible persons for their units, equipped with
              dedicated or part-time safety personnel to implement specific tasks. Frontline teams and position-based
              employees strictly follow operating procedures and carry out hazard self-inspections and mutual checks.
              The Group’s safety management system achieves hierarchical safety responsibilities, ensuring coverage
              of all staff and end-to-end control.


              As of the end of the reporting period, the Group’s six subsidiaries have obtained ISO 45001 Occupational
              Health and Safety Management System certification and passed the annual audit during the reporting
              period.

         7.2 Strict Risk Control
              To eliminate or reduce potential risks in the safety production process, the Group has established the
              Hazard Identification and Hierarchical Control Management System. The system requires relevant
              responsible departments to comprehensively identify, classify, and evaluate health and safety hazard
              factors in production activities, equipment and facilities, raw materials and products, safety protections,
              processes, and other activities, implement corresponding control management, and take targeted
              preventive measures to effectively curb major and catastrophic production safety incidents. In the past
              three years, the Group has not had any work-related fatalities.




     Tiangong International Company Limited
